# Findings ledger

One row per finding, kept for ever. `id` is `A<area>-<n>`. Status: `open`, `fixed <commit>`, or
`accepted: <reason>`. The fixture column names the fixture in the suite once fixed, or holds the
reviewer's words and literals until then. Rounds before this ledger are recorded in
`Docs/FunctionalityVerification/15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md`.

| id | area | severity | finding | fixture | status | found | closed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| A2-1 | 2 | P3 | `ldc Rm,SR` and `ldc.l @Rm+,SR` keep SR bits the hardware does not have: the spec masks with `0x0FFF0FFF` (`superh.sinc`, `ldc rm_08_11,sr` and `ldc.l @rm_08_11+,sr`), Mednafen with `0x3F3` (T S I3-I0 Q M; `sh7095_ops.inc:1653-1664` and `1673-1686`, every SR load ends `z->SR &= 0x3F3`). | A2-2 | 2 | P2 | `rte` restores SR after its delay slot; the hardware restores it before. The spec runs `delayslot(1); pc = _pc; sr = _sr;` (`superh.sinc`, `rte`); Mednafen sets `z->SR` from the stack, then calls `DelayBranch` (`sh7095_ops.inc:1735-1757`, SR at line 1746). So a slot that reads SR (`movt`, `addc`, `subc`, `div1`, a conditional...) sees the old T, S, Q, M, and a slot that writes SR (`sett`, `clrt`, `cmp`, `shll`, `ldc`, `div0s`, ...) has its write overwritten by the stacked value. The usual `rte ; nop` is unaffected; `rte` with a useful slot is the pattern. 1,159 of 2,000 `rte` cases differ (SR in 750 with an ordinary slot, a register in about 60, memory in 13, MAC in 2). Coordinator: re-triaged from P1: wrong only for an rte slot that reads or writes T; a spec fix, area 2 round 1. | Fixed by patch 0019 (`sr = _sr` before `delayslot(1)`, as 0006 did for PR). `SaturnExplorerSh2DecodeTests`: shapes `rte; sett`, `rte; clrt`, `rte; movt r2`, form `rte`. Mutation (SR after the slot): the three shapes fail, 371 of 2,000 `rte` cases. | fixed 4d37623 | area 2 oracle | area 2 round 1 |

| A2-4 | 2 | P3 | A PC-relative load or `mova` in the delay slot of a taken `bt/s`, `bf/s`, `jmp`, `jsr`, `rts`, `braf`, `bsrf` or `rte` reads relative to the slot's own address + 4; the hardware reads PC as the branch target + 2 (`sh7095.inc:4757-4777` `UCDelayBranch` and `4696-4725` `DelayBranch` set PC to the target, the post-switch `z->PC += 2` at `sh7095_ops.inc:2082` follows, and `MOV.W/L @(disp,PC)` and `MOVA` use that PC: `sh7095_ops.inc:109-126, 521`). | A2-5 | 2 | P1 | Translating an instruction can return the p-code of its delay slot instead of its own. `Sleigh` keeps parse trees in a ring of 2 (`DisassemblyCache`, `sleigh.cc:576-582`: `parser_cachesize = 2` because SH-2's `delayslot(1)` is one byte of slot, so `maxdelayslotbytes` is 1; lookups hash on `offset & 31`). If a branch is translated while its tree is cached but its slot's is not, the slot lookup takes the ring's next entry, and that entry is the branch's own tree (`getParserContext` does not advance the ring on a hit). The branch is then built from the slot's parse: `rts` becomes its slot alone, `bt/s` loses the condition and the jump, with no warning. The trigger sequence is: the slot address translated first (as a branch target, say), then the branch, then one other instruction, then the branch again (`872, 870, 874, 870` with `bt/s` at 870 and `sett` at 872: 7 p-code ops, then 3). It also came up unprompted in 1 of 60,000 random `bt/s` executions. Every delayed branch form (`bra`, `bsr`, `braf`, `bsrf`, `jmp`, `jsr`, `rts`, `rte`, `bt/s`, `bf/s`: 5,632 cells) shows it. A vendored-engine defect, not the spec; whether the decompiler's flow can reach the sequence (it needs a slot that is also a jump target and a second translation of the branch, as when flow runs again after a jump table is recovered) is not established, so P1 only by the rule for a silent wrong result; the coordinator may re-triage. Areas 4 and 10 should look at it. Coordinator: kept P1 until its reach is settled: area 10 (patch review) establishes whether flow following can translate a cached branch after a slot miss; if it can, patch the ring size; if it cannot, mark accepted with the proof. | Settled: reachable from the decompiler, fixed by patch 0020 (the cache is least recently used: a hit makes the entry the newest). `ReturnCheck` (`Sh2Decompiler.cpp`) translates a function again after the flow; a function that starts with a delayed branch whose slot is also a jump target, with a loop back to it, translated slot, branch, one plain instruction, branch, and the branch came out as its slot alone: the signature was withheld ("an indirect jump it does not resolve"). The flow itself translates each address once. `SaturnExplorerSh2DecodeTests`: `TestRecycledTreeInDecompiler` (the function, words `A002 7201 412B 0009 8BFA 012A 89F9 89F9 000B 0009` at `06009000`) and `TestTranslationHistory` (5,632 cells). Without the patch: the signature is withheld and 5,632 of 5,632 cells differ. A larger ring is no fix (a tree can always be the oldest). Record: "Area 2, round 1", section 5. | fixed 4d37623 | area 2 oracle (found by a stray `next` difference in the random run) | area 2 round 1 |

| A5-3 | 5 | P2 | An access narrower than the register it reaches is printed as a write or read of that part of the register, with no note, where the hardware does something else. A byte write to a VDP2 or VDP1 register writes the whole 16-bit register, its other byte taken from whatever was last on the data bus (Mednafen `vdp2.c` `RW_W8` -> `RegsWrite(A, *DB)`; `vdp1.c` `VDP1_Write8_DB` -> `WriteReg((A - 0x100000) >> 1, DB)`; `sh7095.inc` `SH7095_BSC_BusWrite_u8` keeps the other byte lane of `DB`). The SH-2's 32-bit on-chip modules (0xFFFFFF00-0xFFFFFFFF: divider, DMAC, BSC) do not take a word as half a register: `OnChipRegWrite_u16` writes the whole of DVDNT at 0xFF04 and starts the division (`SH7095_DIVU_S32_S32`), and writes nothing at 0xFF06; `OnChipRegRead_INLINE_u16` returns the low 16 bits for 0xFF04 and 0xFF06 alike; a byte access raises a CPU address error (`SH7095_SetPEX(z, SH7095_PEX_CPUADDR)`) and writes the byte doubled. `HardwareNotes` compares widths only for the keyed watchdog and for an access covering two registers, although the comment on the disabled rules in `Sh2Decompiler.cpp` says "VDP2 refuses byte writes". The ledger is right (the instruction's width); the line's meaning is not. P2 as a missing caution; the coordinator may read it as "wrong width with no warning" (P1): the printed width is the instruction's, the effect's is the register's. Corpus: no wrong instance (the only partial register accesses are `SH2_BCR1._2_2_`, word reads at 0xFFFFFFE2 in 2 functions of each save state, which Mednafen answers with the low half, as printed). | Words from 0x06009000, the pool among them; `Fixture` literal offset and value. **a** (VDP2 byte write): `{ 0x06009000, { 0xD102, 0xE003, 0x2100, 0x000B, 0x0009, 0x0009, 0x25F8, 0x0001 }, 0x0C, 0x25F80001 }` (`mov.l @(pool),r1 ; mov #3,r0 ; mov.b r0,@r1`). Expected: the same ledger and a caution on 0x06009004 that VDP2 takes no byte write (the 16-bit VDP2_TVMD is written whole, its other byte undefined). Literal 0x25F80000 the same (`VDP2_TVMD._0_1_ = 3;`). **b** (VDP1): literal 0x25D00001: `VDP1_TVMR._1_1_ = 3;`, no note; expected the same caution. **c** (on-chip word write): `{ 0x06009000, { 0xD102, 0xE003, 0x2101, 0x000B, 0x0009, 0x0009, 0xFFFF, 0xFF06 }, 0x0C, 0xFFFFFF06 }`: `SH2_DVDNT._2_2_ = 3;`, no note; expected a caution that the 32-bit register takes no 16-bit write there. Fixed: `HardwareRegister::narrow` (`NarrowAccess`) in the map's access-kind table: VDP1 and VDP2 registers `WholeOnByte`, 32-bit on-chip registers from 0xFFFFFF00 `OnChipLong` (the bus state controller `OnChipKeyedLong`), 16-bit ones there `OnChipWord`; `NarrowAccessEffects` says what the access does and `HardwareNotes` adds it as a caution on the instruction, naming the register and the width. | A5-4 | 5 | P2 | A 16-bit write at the even address of an SMPC register writes that register, and the output calls it RAM. The SMPC is a byte device on the low byte lane: Mednafen `ss.c` `BusRW_DB_CS0_u16_W1` does `SMPC_A = (A & 0x7F) >> 1` and a `SMPC_Write(...)` that no `A & 1` test guards, so `mov.w r0,@r1` at 0x2010001E writes SMPC_COMREG with r0's low byte: an SMPC command. The map names SMPC registers 1 byte wide at the odd addresses only, so the line is `Ram2010001e = 0x19;` with no note. Same decode: a byte write at an even SMPC address does nothing (`BusRW_DB_CS0_u8_W1` writes only when `A & 1`), printed as a write with no note; a 32-bit write at 0x2010001C is two 16-bit bus writes (SMPC register 0x0E, then COMREG), printed `Ram2010001c = 0x19;`; a 16-bit read at 0x20100060 returns `0xFF00` ORed with SMPC_SR (`BusRW_DB_CS0_u16_W0`), printed `Ram20100060`. The markup is silently cut short, something later in the Print phase fails on it with an exception that is none of `std::bad_alloc`, `std::exception` or `LowlevelError` (it is caught by the final `catch (...)`), and `Decompile` reports `Failed` with the text `unexpected exception` instead of `out of memory`. The engine is still marked damaged (the `catch (...)` arm sets it), so the process is safe and the next request is Unavailable; only the message is wrong. 8 allocations of 513 bytes (the stream's buffer growth): mailbox 21067, 21073, 32980, 32988; jumptable 31762, 31771, 31774, 31779. | `kPins`: mailbox print #0 513 B #0 and 1025 B #0, jumptable print #0 513 B #0; removing the host's `markup.exceptions(badbit)` fails these 3 cells with "unexpected exception" | fixed d41c806 | area 6 oracle (`review/6-lifecycle`) | area 6 round 1 |
| A6-5 | 6 | P2 | The same stream behaviour in the Actions and Flow phases hides an allocation failure completely: Ghidra builds warning text and messages in `std::ostringstream` (`Funcdata::removeUnreachableBlocks`, `funcdata_block.cc:408`; `FlowInfo::recoverJumpTables`, `flow.cc`) and the failed 513-byte buffer growth only truncates the text. `Decompile` returns `Ok`, no engine damage is recorded, nothing is reported, and the output is wrong: mailbox allocation 18426 turns `/* WARNING: Removing unreachable block (ram,0x0600a48c) */` into `/* WARNING: Removing unreac */` in the shown pseudocode (the caution a reader relies on to learn that code was removed). Allocations 9188 (mailbox) and 8305, 20591 (jumptable) are swallowed the same way with the output unchanged this time, which is luck. Fix: set `exceptions(std::ios::badbit)` on the engine's message streams (a patch to the few `ostringstream` sites that feed comments and warnings), or have the host reject a result whose warning text is cut. | `kPins`: mailbox actions #0 and #1 513 B #0 (#1 is the truncated `WARNING: Removing unreac`), jumptable followFlow #0 and #1 513 B #0; reverting `error.hh`/`filemanage.hh` (the engine's `ostringstream`) fails these 4 cells | fixed d41c806 | area 6 oracle (`review/6-lifecycle`) | area 6 round 1 |
| A6-6 | 6 | P3 | A cancel raised in the tail of the first pass's Actions is carried into the second pass. `Sh2Decompiler.cpp` documents that a cancel "requested anywhere in a phase is then honoured in that phase, never carried into the next one's work", and does check at the end of Flow and before Print, but the first pass does not print, so `runPass` returns before its `checkCancel()`. The flag waits through the second pass's Teardown and Init (Architecture construction has no check-point: about 7,970 of 19,913 allocations of the fixture1 run, 40%) and is honoured in Flow of the second pass: `cancelledIn` is Flow for a flag raised in Actions. Found on fixture1 (allocations 9897 and 9898, the last two before the first Teardown) and on mailbox by the timer family (48,400 us into the run, ASan). Cost: one extra Architecture build before a Stop or a budget cancel takes effect. Fix: a `checkCancel()` before `return std::string()` in the non-printing branch. | default subset, `cancel-alloc fixture1` around the first pass's Teardown entry (allocations 10662-10663 in the numbering of this commit); removing the `checkCancel()` of the pass boundary fails these 2 cells | fixed d41c806 | area 6 oracle (`review/6-lifecycle`) | area 6 round 1 |
| A6-7 | 6 | P3 | An allocation failure while an `Architecture` is being built or initialised leaks what was built so far. Under ASan with leak detection 2,742 of 3,877 sampled cells of the mailbox fixture and 2,705 of 3,610 of the jumptable one (every cell with the failure in an Init phase, allocations 27 to about 8,000 of each Architecture) leak between a few hundred bytes and about 30 KB. Bounded: the engine is refused after the first in-engine failure, so at most one such leak per process. | A2-9 | 2 | P1 | `trapa` is translated as a plain call, so in the decompiler's view the 8 bytes it pushes are never popped: every stack access after a `trapa` is 8 bytes off and reads what `trapa` pushed. The spec (`superh.sinc:1886-1897`, `trapa`) does `r15 -= 4; *r15 = sr; r15 -= 4; *r15 = inst_next; call [*(vbr + imm*4)]`, and the default prototype keeps r15 across a call (`superh.cspec`: r15 in `<unaffected>`, `extrapop="0"`), so after it r15 is the entry value minus 8. On the hardware the handler returns with `rte`, which pops PC and SR (`sh7095_ops.inc:1739-1743`, `z->R[15] += 4` twice) after `TRAPA` (`sh7095_ops.inc:1900-1906`) pushed them through `Exception` (`sh7095.inc:4809-4813`, `z->R[15] -= 4` twice): r15 is back where it was. So a register saved before a system call and restored after it reads back the return address `trapa` pushed, a word just above the stack pointer reads the stacked SR, and the output says nothing about it (the only text is the withheld signature's "an indirect call it does not resolve", which is about lending, not the body). The instruction itself is right: the differential run agrees on the two pushes, their order (SR first), the pushed PC (`inst_next`), the vector read (VBR + imm*4) and the access widths. Reach: code that saves registers around a `trapa` (a system-call wrapper); rare in Saturn games, which call the BIOS through `jsr`. | Fixed by patch 0025. `Sh2DecompilerTests` `TestCallsAndTraps`: the ledger's fixtures a and b word for word and `50F1`, a trap with a local, two traps in a row, the entry r15; each also run on the p-code emulator (handler `rte ; nop`) and the evaluated return compared with r0. `Sh2SemanticsTests` `TestTrapaStack`. Mutation (the pop removed): every trapa fixture and oracle check fails; the decode test, the semantics test and the generator pass (the emulator ends an instruction at its call and never runs the op, so the differential run cannot see it). Record: `15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md`, "Areas 2 and 3, round 2". | fixed 5944580 | area 2 round 2 review | areas 2 and 3 round 2 |
| A2-10 | 2 | P1 | A register the default prototype neither keeps nor kills reads, after a call, as the value it had before the call. `superh.cspec` names r2 and r3 `killedbycall` (r0 and r1 are the output) and r8-r15 and GBR `unaffected`; r4-r7, SR (T, and S, Q, M), MACH and MACL are in neither list, so the engine gives the call an unknown effect on them: it does not fold the old value through the call, but it merges the values before and after into one variable, and the C text then reads as if the call left them alone (`xVar1 = 5; func(); return xVar1;`). A callee may change every one of them: any `clrt`, `sett` or compare writes T (`sh7095_ops.inc:1636-1639` `CLRT`, `SH7095_SetT` in each compare), any `mov` writes r4-r7 (`MOV_IMM_REG`, lines 96-104), and r4-r7 and T are scratch in the SH calling conventions. A hand-written routine that returns a status in T (`bsr check ; nop ; bt fail`) is the common shape, and the output then shows the branch decided by the T from before the call. A listed register shows what is wanted: r2 prints `return extraout_r2;`, an unknown. Lent prototypes do not change it: `ApplyPrototype` (`Sh2Decompiler.cpp:179-191`) sets `pieces.model = arch.defaultfp`, the same effect lists. Whether a callee may change MACH and MACL is an ABI question; they print the same way. A calling-convention matter (area 3 may own the fix in the cspec), listed here because T is an SR flag the ledger cannot see. | Fixed by patch 0026: r0, r1, r4-r7, MACH, MACL and SR in `killedbycall`, VBR in `unaffected`. `Sh2DecompilerTests` `TestCallsAndTraps`: the ledger's a and b word for word, r4-r6, MACL, MACH and r1 (with a lent void callee), recursion through r1 and r7 (round-3 addendum), VBR across a call, lent prototypes, controls (r2, r8, an argument kept in r8) and a pinned limit (a callee that keeps r4 is not told apart); the oracle runs each on the emulator. Mutation: each list entry dropped fails the fixtures for it. Corpus: 87 of 839 and 136 of 1,554 functions differ, 74 and 112 call sites gain an argument. Open consequence: a caller relying on a callee keeping r4-r7 reads `extraout_r4` and is not lent a signature (needs per-callee effects from the engine; see the record). Record: `15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md`, "Areas 2 and 3, round 2". | fixed 5944580 | area 2 round 2 review | areas 2 and 3 round 2 |

A closed panel asks nothing, so when the view returns to the same identity before the panel is shown again (scrub a recorded frame and resume the live view, or scrub away and back to the same frame; a BIOS image dropped and chosen again; a function start set and cleared), `q == mAsked` in `Update` and nothing is submitted. The function the user chose never appears. The previous one stays on screen with the stale badge, whose tooltip says "its replacement is shown when it is ready". There is no "decompiling...", no note, and nothing outstanding, until Enter or Refresh. While the panel is shown this cannot happen: the `Update` that discards the answer asks the new question at once. | `SE_MODEL_SCRIPT="hold; select A; hide; scrub 1; wait; resume; show; wait" SE_MODEL_WORLD=C,A build/bin/SaturnExplorerDecompilerModelTests <scratch>`: NOT REFRESHED after operation 7 (shown C, wanted A, pending 0, discarded 1). | A3-3 | 3 | P2 | A lent callee proved to change a register the default model holds unaffected (r8-r14, r15, GBR, VBR) is still taken to keep it. `ReturnCheck` clears the register's bit in `CallEffects.preserved`, and `LendEffects` (patch 0028) only marks kept registers among r2-r7, SR, MACH and MACL unaffected. It never kills a register the proof shows changed. So the caller reads the value from before the call, and the caller's own signature is lent on that value. Without lending, the same text is the cspec's convention (r8-r14 and GBR callee-saved). Once lent, the host holds a proof to the contrary and ignores it. Hand-written Saturn routines use custom conventions. Corpus image 008, lending run with callees first (as `TestGameLending`): `060647EC` (`mov #0x40,r0 ; add r14,r0 ; mov.w @r8+,r1 ; mov.w @r8+,r2 ; shll8 r1 ; mov.w @r8+,r3 ...`, which takes r8 and r14 and advances r8) is lent `void f(void)` and called in a loop by `0606538E`, itself lent. `06064228` (`mov.l @(lit),r0 ; ldc r0,gbr ; ... mov.b r0,@(d,gbr)`) sets GBR and is lent to `060641E0`, which is lent to `06063F2E`. Of 1,004 lent prototypes, 295 miss one of these registers. Most are r14/r15 of entries found at a `sts.l pr,@-r15` one instruction after the real prologue (`mov.l r14,@-r15`), an artifact of that heuristic. 23 callers are lent such a callee. | `TestCallsAndTraps`, "A3-3 (round 3)": `r8 set by the callee`, `r9 ...`, `r14 ...` (the caller reads `extraout_rN` and is withheld on it; with the effects not lent, by the test hook, it prints `return 5;`), `r8 saved and restored by the callee` (proved kept: `return 5;`, lent), `GBR set by the callee`, `VBR set by the callee`, `r15 lowered by the callee` (the callee's constant r15 delta is its extrapop: the caller's pop reads the callee's word, `xStack_8`, and both are lent), `r15 moved by an argument` (the delta varies: not lent, nor its caller). Generator: check 10 over GBR, VBR, MACH and MACL (they start at values of their own), new check 12 (`kept-read`: the text never reads a register a lent callee changes as the value it had on entry), grammar 2 callees that leave r12-r14, GBR, VBR, MACH or MACL changed and callers that read the register right after the call. `TestGameLending` prints the callers of such callees | fixed fdc68ff, b1d783a, fb2a517 (`LendEffects` kills the r8-r14, GBR and VBR a lent callee was proved to change; a constant r15 offset is lent as the callee's extrapop and applied by `ReturnCheck`, one that differs between returns withholds; "Area 3, round 3" in 15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md) | areas 3 and 4 round 3 review | area 3 round 3 fixes |
| A3-4 | 3 | P2 | A callee that writes through a structure-return pointer in r2 is not seen to write the caller's local: the read after the call is folded to the value stored before it, lent or not. The SH ABI of both compilers (Renesas SHC; GCC, `SH_STRUCT_VALUE_REGNUM` 2) passes the address of a returned structure in r2. The cspec has no input in r2, so `mov r15,r2` is dead, the local's address never reaches the call, and the engine takes the call to leave the local alone. The host knows better and does not say so. The callee's `CallEffects.storesThrough` has r2's bit, which is why the caller's `ReturnCheck` (lent) clobbers its saved PR and withholds "a return not to its caller". On `mailbox` it is 840, 842 and 844. Every cell, the pins' census and `--sites` run the next run (N+1), but they index it with the baseline's sizes. `--cell` then prints coordinates (`PIN-AT`) for an allocation it does not fail. When the markup crosses a 1,024-byte decoder buffer between the two runs, every later allocation of Print is off by one. The census then names run N+1's allocation k under the baseline's size at k, and nothing checks that the size matches. So `--sites` reports a 32-byte list node as `endIngest`'s 1-byte buffer, a pin's `site ok` can be another allocation of the same function, and a fixture tuned with `--sites` to reach a path misses it in every cell. That is how this round's endIngest probe first missed. The text is identical across the runs, and so are the tokens. | `Warm` takes the baseline from a forked run N+1 (the run every cell makes); the cell checks that the allocation it fails is the baseline's size; the pins' census runs the baseline's sequence and checks the size at each pin's coordinates and the whole run, size for size; a site names its ordinal (`#n`: the n-th allocation of the pin's size at the site in the phase entry); `--sites` takes a site, lists ordinals and marks an allocation of another size; `--diffruns` shows the markup growing. An allocation failure before any phase entry is outside the host's exception boundary for a damaged engine (`mWorkPhase` is Idle), so the engine was not marked damaged. Three outcomes, by where the allocation fell (swept over every allocation of the first request): (1) inside the materialisation (allocations 0-62 with the bundle extracted, 0-103 without): the first request was Failed("out of memory") and every later one Unavailable with an empty explanation (167 cells); (2) inside `startDecompilerLibrary` (66-506, 107-547): Failed("out of memory") with the engine not damaged, and for allocations 63-106 (index 100, 56 bytes, in `AttributeId::initialize` among them) the next request Failed("No print languages registered") from the half-filled list (6 cells); the others reached the engine with tables of unknown content (882 cells); (3) a Disassemble marked the engine damaged for an out of memory in the materialisation, which touches no engine global. | A5-52 | 5 | P2 | Two addresses of the same work-RAM bytes are two memories to the engine's data flow: a store through one alias is forwarded to a later load through the same alias past a store through another alias, so the load is folded away and its value printed wrong. `xRam26001000 = 5; xRam06001000 = 7; return 5;` where the hardware returns 7: 0x06001000 and 0x26001000 are the same word (the cached and cache-through partitions; `sh7095.inc` `MemWrite` case 0 writes the cache and falls into case 1's `ExtBusWrite`; the final read is through the cache-through partition, so no cache state changes the answer), and so are 0x26100000 and 0x26000000 (high work RAM, `ss.c` `BusRW_DB_CS3_*`: `A & 0xFFFFF`), 0x2E001000 and 0x26001000 (bits 28-27 not decoded), 0x20201000 and 0x00201000 (low work RAM). DESIGN.md 6.1 folds "the HWRAM 1 MiB repeat" for the image; the data flow does not know it. The read is not in the ledger (an access to ordinary memory at a constant address is folded by design) and no oracle compares the printed value: the generator's emulator does alias work RAM (`Env::ReadValue`, `ram[(a + i) & 0xFFFFF]`: the run returns 7), but outside grammar 3 (register-only) nothing compares the value the text returns. A user who marks the range volatile is safe (`VolatileAliases` covers every alias); the default request is not. Corpus: one word named through both partitions in 3 functions (`hwram007` 06003C46 and 060340FE, `hwram008` 0607513E, the slave's idle loop: `xRam26003304 = 0; ... Ram46003304 = 0; ... while (pcRam06003304 == (code *)0x0)`, and `hwram008` 060716A8: `(*pcRam260b72e8)(); xRam060b72e8 = 0;`); none forwards a store across the alias, so no corpus line is wrong today. (The idle loop is right by accident: an engine that knew the alias would fold the poll to the 0 stored before it, the mailbox case, unless the purge at 0x46003304 is taken as the line's barrier.) Possibly the engine's memory model (areas 2/4); filed here as the mirror map's. | `a5_52a`, `a5_52_repeat`, `a5_52_bit27`, `a5_52_low`, `a5_52_long`, `a5_52_same`, `a5_52_apart`, `a5_52_idle`, `a5_52_purge`; generator rule 14 (`alias.words`) and `SE_GENERATED_MUTATE=alias` | fixed 90d80c9 (follow-ups 3a71c6f, 6802560, 17d8e27, 3376de3) | area 5 round 7 review | area 5 round 7 fix |
| A5-53 | 5 | P3 | A5-50's neighbours: VDP memory with aliases the data flow does not know, so a store is forwarded across a write to the same entry as in A5-52. VDP2 colour RAM repeats every 4 KiB through 0x25F00000-0x25F7FFFF (`vdp2.c` `RW_W16`/`RW_R16`: `cri = (A & 0xFFF) >> 1`), and in colour RAM mode 0 a write of entry n writes entry n + 0x400 too (`case CRAM_MODE_RGB555_1024: (CRAM + 0x000)[cri & 0x3FF] = *DB; (CRAM + 0x400)[cri & 0x3FF] = *DB;`), at either width, so 0x25F00800 is 0x25F00000 in mode 0; VDP2 VRAM repeats every 512 KiB through 0x25E00000-0x25EFFFFF (`(A & 0x7FFFF) >> 1`); the VDP1 frame buffer every 256 KiB (`VDP1_Write16_DB`: `(FBA >> 1) & 0x1FFFF`). A fix for A5-50 that is about byte writes only will not reach the word form. The narrow writes A5-50 asked about are right elsewhere, against the bus handlers: VDP2 VRAM `(VRAM[vri] & ~mask) or (*DB & mask)` and VDP1 VRAM and frame buffer (one byte stored) print `CONCAT11(old, v)`, which is what they store; sound RAM is volatile and printed as the byte write it is (`SOUND_Write8`, `SCSP_NE16_RW(uint8_t ...)`); the SCU DSP ports take `*DB` whole and have the `PortWritesWhole` caution; VDP1/VDP2 registers the `ByteWritesWord` caution. | A2-31 | 2 | P2 | Whether a value left in r0 is returned depends on what sits in `rts`'s delay slot. `nop` is `r0 = r0` in the spec (`superh.sinc`, `:nop`: "FIXME: intentional nop ... do this to suppress warning"), and that COPY is a top ancestor whose only use is the RETURN, so `Funcdata::ancestorOpUse`/`onlyOpUse` (`funcdata_varnode.cc:1916-2090`, upstream) accept the return trial. With any other slot (a callee-saved pop `mov.l @r15+,r14`, `add #n,r15`, `sett`, `mov r0,r1` ...) the trial is the value's own definition, and `onlyOpUse` rejects it when the value has any other use: a CBRANCH (via `cmp/eq #imm,r0`, `tst r0,r0`, `cmp/pz r0`), a LOAD or STORE address or a stored value, a CALL, a copy to r1 that reaches the RETURN; `ancestorOpUse` also returns false outright for a CALL output ("a call is never a good indication"). The return is then dropped, the text prints `void`, and the signature is **lent** `void`, often with the parameter that was the value dropped too. SH compilers fill the slot of the epilogue with the last pop, and r0 is the only register `cmp/eq #imm` and `tst #imm` take, so the shapes are ordinary compiled code: `return g(p);`, `p = g(x); if (p) *p = 0; return p;`, `return ++*p;`, `if (x == 5) *p = v; return x;`. The binary of a `void` function that leaves such a value in r0 is the same, so which reading is right is not decidable from the code; the defect is that an unrelated slot instruction decides it and the answer is lent as known (A3-5/A3-14's decision for a dropped r0 is "withheld"). Both oracles miss it: the generator's returning functions never return a call's result or a compared/stored r0 through a filled slot (grammar 2, 3,000 seeds: 0 findings, "slot pops=1997"), and the printed-C differential counts the leaf forms `void-return` (A2-34) and has no calls. Corpus: image 008 with lending (`SE_GAME_LEND`), 131 functions (some entries twice, at +0 and +2) print `void` and end in `func_0x...(...); return;` to a callee whose own text returns a value, every one with a non-nop rts slot; 41 of them lent. With the slot replaced by a nop and nothing else changed, 324 of 397 `void` functions of image 007 and 778 of 963 of image 008 print a return (`slotscan`, scratch). Mednafen: `NOP` (`sh7095_ops.inc`) does nothing. Neighbours: A3-19, A3-20 (dropped returns, lent `void`, other mechanisms). | **a** (wrapper, compiled shape): `SE_GENERATED_WORDS="2FE6 4F22 B003 0009 4F26 000B 6EF6 6042 000B 0009" SE_GENERATED_FUNCS="0/0/e,e/e/14" SE_GENERATED_RETURNS="1,1" build/bin/SaturnExplorerSh2GeneratedTests <dir>` (`mov.l r14,@-r15 ; sts.l pr,@-r15 ; bsr g ; nop ; lds.l @r15+,pr ; rts ; mov.l @r15+,r14`; g at 0600900E is `mov.l @r4,r0 ; rts ; nop`, lent `known, 1 param(s), returns`). Current: `void func_0x06009000(xunknown4 *param_1) { func_0x0600900e(param_1); return; }`, `prototype: known, 1 param(s), void`; generator `[n:return-dropped]`. Control: `2FE6 4F22 B004 0009 4F26 6EF6 000B 0009 6042 000B 0009` (`FUNCS="0/0/10,10/10/16"`, the pop before `rts`, `nop` in the slot): `xVar1 = func_0x06009010(param_1); return xVar1;`, lent `returns`, 0 findings. As a `CallFixture`: caller `{ 0x06009000, { 0x2FE6, 0x4F22, 0xB003, 0x0009, 0x4F26, 0x000B, 0x6EF6 } }`, callee at `0600900E` `{ 0x6042, 0x000B, 0x0009 }`. **b** (`p = g(x); if (p) *p = 0; return p;`): `2FE6 4F22 B008 0009 2008 8901 E100 2012 4F26 000B 6EF6 0009 6042 000B 0009`, `FUNCS="0/0/16,18/18/1e" RETURNS="1,1"`: `void`, lent, `return-dropped`; control with `4F26 6EF6 000B 0009` returns `pxVar1`. **c** leaves, one word apart (`7F00` = `add #0,r15`, no effect): `6042 7001 2402 000B 7F00` (`return ++*p`) `void func(int4 *param_1)`, lent; `6043 2052 000B 7F00` `void`, lent, where `6043 2052 000B 0009` returns `param_1`; `6043 8805 8B00 2652 000B 7F00` (`if (x == 5) *p = v; return x;`) `void`, 3 params, lent, where `... 000B 0009` returns `param_1`; `6053 7001 000B 6103` (`rts ; mov r0,r1`, the A2-22 copy-to-r1 shape) `void func_0x06009000(void)`, 0 params, lent; generator with `FUNCS="0/0/8" RETURNS="1"`: `return-dropped`. Expected: the slot does not decide the return (no `r0 = r0` in `nop`, or the copy marked incidental) and, where the engine's heuristic drops an r0 the function writes on every path, the signature withheld, not lent `void`. Oracle: generator check 9 (`return-dropped`) rejects every fixture given the intent. Fixed as `0045-sleigh-nop-writes-nothing-rte-sr-after-slot.patch` (`nop` writes nothing: it was `r0 = r0`, the last write of r0 and its only use, so a function ending `rts ; nop` returned its value and the same function with any other instruction in the slot did not) and `0044-engine-round5-printed-value-fixes.patch` (1) (`Funcdata::ancestorOpUse`: a RETURN of the output of a call whose prototype is locked and returns a value is a return, whatever else the value is used for; upstream's "a call is never a good indication of a single op use" is a rule for parameters and decided the return of a wrapper). The rest of the decision is in "Area 2, round 5" in 15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md: the slot no longer decides and the engine's own rule (a value with another use is scratch) holds in every slot; withholding every `void` signature over an r0 written on every path was measured and rejected (lent signatures 600 -> 365 in image 007 and 984 -> 553 in image 008); what is left is A2-42. The nop had also hidden a class the host never withheld, r0 computed from r0's own entry value and then used again (`shll r0 ; cmp/eq #1,r0 ; rts`): `InputsInReturn` now withholds it in every slot, for any value computed from r0's entry value (`add r4,r0 ; xor r0,r1 ; rts`, generator seed 12623, has a parameter in it too), and `xor r0,r0` / `x - x` are 0, not a read (`Sh2Decompiler.cpp`, `ReturnCheck::Return` and its interpretation of two ops; the corpus's one casualty, image 008's 06073C7C, was the zeroing idiom). Fixtures: `TestArea2Round5` (`return g(p)` with the pop in the slot and with a nop, `p = g(x); if (p) *p = 0; return p;`, `++*p` and a compare in two slots, `shll r0` in two slots, controls); the generator's `return g(p)` form (`fn.ret_call`, grammar 2); the printed-C intent check of A2-34. | fixed 350c98f | area 2 round 5 review | area 2 round 5 fix |

| A5-63 | 5 | P3 | (Area 6's invariant, found through A5-52's retry.) A cancel raised in the teardown between two passes of an alias retry is carried through the next pass's Init into its Flow instead of being honoured in Actions (A6-35: a cancel is honoured where it was raised, never carried into the next phase). The first pass has its `checkCancel()` after its locals and Architecture are gone; the retry (`aliasRetry`, a printing pass that found words named twice returns before it prints and gives way to another) has none, because `runPass` returns the empty string and the loop starts the next pass. A request that has a first pass (`registerCallees`) finds the words in it and never retries, which is why the common case and the fault suite's fixtures are right; a request without one (`registerCallees = false`, the fault suite's `lean`, or a seeded request) retries once per growth of the set. Fault suite cell on the fixture below: `FAIL cancel-enter aliasl 3: invariant: the cancel is honoured where the design says -- raised in teardown#3: expected Cancelled-in-Actions but got status 2 cancelledIn followFlow` (phases: init, followFlow, actions, teardown, init, followFlow, actions, print, teardown; cells 0-2 and 4-8 pass, the non-lean fixture with the same words passes all nine). The default subset has no fixture that retries. The result is still `Cancelled` and carries no lines: the phase named is wrong, and a hook raising at that entry sees the next pass's Init start. P3. A check-point after the pass's teardown for a retried pass, as for the first, closes it. Fixed in the host: `DecompileInto` calls `checkCancel()` after a printing pass that gives way to another (`aliasRetry`), as after the first pass (A6-35), so a cancel raised in that teardown is honoured in Actions. Fault suite: fixtures `aliasl` (lean, retries) and `alias` (first pass, no retry) in the default subset's cancel-enter family; the alloc sweeps run on them too. | A5-68 | 5 | P3 | (Corrects A5-57's premise, and adds the case it was reaching for.) The VDP1 frame buffer's 8 bpp rotate address scramble does not make two addresses one byte, or one address two: `FBA = (FBA and 0x1FF) or ((FBA << 1) and 0x3FC00) or ((FBA >> 8) and 0x200)` is a permutation of the 18 address bits (checked over all 2^18 offsets: no collisions, and the 512 KiB window repeats it exactly as the 256 KiB repeat of the plain mode does), bit 0 stays in place so a word keeps its two bytes, and a byte written at A is read back from A in either mode. So within a mode a store forwarded to a load of the same address is right, and A5-53's `MemoryBytesOf` repeat is exact for both modes; the claim that a forwarded store 'can still be wrong' there does not hold. What does break the data flow is a register write between the store and the load that re-addresses the memory: `VDP1_TVMR` (`vdp1.c` `WriteReg` case 0: `VDP1_TVMR = value and 0xF`, immediate; the scramble applies to every later CPU access) and `VDP2_RAMCTL` (`vdp2.c` `RegsWrite` case 0x0E: `CRAM_Mode = (V >> 12) and 0x3`, immediate; modes 2 and 3 map entry `cri` to `((cri >> 1) and 0x3FF) or ((cri and 1) << 10)`, so an entry stored in mode 0 is not where mode 3 reads it). Both fold today: `xRam25c80200 = 5; VDP1_TVMR = 3; return 5;` where the load reads the byte the plain mode stored at 0x400 of the buffer, not the 5 (offset 0x200 is 0x400 scrambled); `xRam25f00004 = 0x1234; VDP2_RAMCTL = 0x3000; return 0x1234;` where mode 3 reads entry 1, which the mode 0 store never wrote. The same class as A5-52 (the data flow keys memory by address and the address means something else after the write), at the price of a rare shape: P3. Fix: a write to either register makes the memories it re-addresses volatile in the function, or says it (A5-57 proposed the same). The decision on A5-57 is the coordinator's: its text calls a bijection an alias. Fixed (the first of A5-57's two proposals): a write to VDP1_TVMR or VDP2_RAMCTL anywhere in the function makes every access it makes to the memory the register re-addresses (the frame buffer, colour RAM) volatile in that function (`ConstAccess::remaps`, `NewAliasedRanges`), so no store is carried across the write. A barrier by position was not taken: the data flow has no hook for one, and a function that writes a mode register while using the memory is rare. | `a5_68_tvmr`, `a5_68_ramctl` | fixed 34806ec | area 5 round 8 | area 5 round 8 fix |
| A6-40 | 6 | P3 | `SaturnArchitecture::StartLibrary` treats a failure of the spec materialisation that is an exception (an allocation) as transient (A6-30: the stage stays Fresh and the next request starts again) and every other failure as an outcome for the whole process: `bundle.ok == false` sets `Stage::Refused`, `firstError` is returned to every later request and nothing retries (the header: "Once per process: later calls return the first outcome"). A cause that is gone one request later -- a configuration directory that could not be created or written (a full disk, a descriptor limit at the lock file, a permission the user then fixes) and, on Windows by reading, a staged directory a virus scanner still holds when `MovePath` renames it -- leaves the decompiler Unavailable until Saturn Explorer is restarted, and the refusal text ("Decompiler unavailable: cannot create <dir>/decompiler", "cannot move ...", "cannot write ...") does not say that a restart is what clears it, as the Interrupted text does ("restart Saturn Explorer to decompile again"). Nothing global is written while the stage is Fresh (the stage comment says so) and `Extract` clears its own staging directory first, so a retry is safe; its cost is the verification of the bundle on a request that would be refused again. Found by asking of every refusal whether it is retried or explained. | `refused-retry` (`RefusedRetryCell`, a job of the default subset and the full run, a process of its own; by hand `<scratch> --refused-retry`): a regular file where the configuration directory goes (Unavailable, "cannot create ...cfg/decompiler"), the same again (the same text), a Disassemble (the reason as one line), the file replaced by a directory holding a file where `decompiler` goes (another refusal), that file removed (Ok, then Ok again and a listing). Reverting `StartLibrary` to the Refused stage: the job fails on the fourth request (still Unavailable) and the listing | fixed 5f604c2 (test 6296463): a failure of the spec materialisation, of any kind, is a failure of that request only. `StartLibrary` no longer has a Refused stage: `MaterialiseSh2SpecBundle` writes nothing the engine reads, so a failure (a directory or file that cannot be made, a lock, a rename a scanner holds, an allocation) leaves the stage Fresh, answers this request Unavailable with the reason, and the next request starts again; the cost is a directory check or one verified extraction on a request the user made, and nothing is rate-limited (a clock would make which request is retried depend on timing). Interrupted stays the one permanent refusal and says that a restart is what clears it. DECOMPILER_GUIDE.md says "fix it and press Refresh" | area 6 round 5 | area 6 round 5 |
| A6-41 | 6 | P3 | Oracle strength. The host's own check-points at the phase boundaries are pinned by very few cells, and since A6-35 widened the thread family's allowed set they are invisible to `cancel-us`: a raise read in the Actions of the printing pass may be Ok, a raise read in Flow may be Cancelled-in-Actions, a raise in the first pass's Teardown may be either. Only the exact families (`cancel-alloc`, `cancel-enter`) see them, and only when a raise lands between the engine's last check-point and the boundary. Mutation M1 (the `checkCancel()` before `Enter(DecompilePhase::Print)` in `runPass` removed): the default subset fails one job, `cancel-alloc fixture1` k = 24282..24283 (2 cells, "expected Cancelled-in-Actions but got status 0 cancelledIn idle"); `cancel-enter`, `cancel-us`, `cancel-oom` and every other fixture pass; with the full run's window (+-24 around every entry) `cancel-alloc` on mailbox (1,302 cells) and jumptable (1,079) still passes and only the fixture1 family (fixture1, lend, lendm, lendx: 2 cells each) fails, so the check has no cell on the two fixtures that have a reference run or a table. Mutation M5 (the check before `Enter(Actions)` removed): 13 cells in 3 jobs (jumptable 4, mailbox 4, fixture1 5), `cancel-us` passes. A change to the allocation pattern near the end of fixture1's Actions would leave the default subset (and CI) green with the Actions-to-Print check gone. | `cancel-bound <fixture>` (default subset, the fixtures of `cancel-enter`; `--cell cancel-bound <fixture> <2j | 2j+1>`): the host calls `SetBoundaryHookForTests` immediately before each of its own check-points, a test raises the flag in it (cell 2j) or at the last allocation made before it (cell 2j+1), and the check must honour it in the phase that ends there; one more cell is past the last check-point; `BoundaryCheck` requires the set of check-points to be the one the phase log implies (a Flow that goes on to Actions, an Actions that goes on to Print, an Actions-Teardown that goes on to an Init). | A2-43 | 2 | P3 | The instruction in `rts`'s slot (or the last one before it) still decides whether r0 is returned when it writes r0 with r0's own value: A2-31's `nop` was `r0 = r0`, and the other identity writes of r0 are the same COPY-like op with the same consequence. `mov r0,r0` (`6003`), `and r0,r0` (`2009`), `or r0,r0` (`200B`), `add #0,r0` (`7000`), `xor #0,r0` (`CA00`) and `or #0,r0` (`CB00`) make a new value that is a copy of the r0 that was compared or stored, and whose only use is the RETURN, so the engine returns it and the function is lent `returns`; `nop`, `add #0,r15`, `mov r0,r1`, `sett` leave that r0 as the scratch of A2-42 (accepted) and the function is lent `void`. No compiler emits an identity write of r0 (a real cast in the slot, `extu.b r0,r0`, `exts.w r0,r0`, is a value computed from r0 and is rightly a return), so P3. | A2-44 | 2 | P3 | (Oracle.) The printed-C differential cannot tell a swapped byte name of an unaffected register: `unaff_r8_b1` and `unaff_r8_b2` get the same value. `CalleeSaved(r)` is `0xA0000000 + r * 0x01010101`, so its bytes 1 and 2 are equal for every r8-r14 (r8: `A8 08 08 08`), and the harness reads `unaff_rN_bK` as `(CalleeSaved(N) >> 8K) & 0xFF`. A mutant of `registerPieceSuffix` (`database.cc`, patch 0044 (3)) that numbers the bytes 1 and 2 the other way round (`if (lsb == 1) lsb = 2; else if (lsb == 2) lsb = 1;` before `if (lsb != 0)`) prints `(char)((uint4)param_3 >> 8) == unaff_r8_b2` and `(char)((uint4)param_3 >> 0x10) == unaff_r8_b1`, the wrong byte of the register under each name, and the printed-C differential says `agrees` for `cmp/str r6,r8` and `cmp/str r6,r14`, and the unit suite's A2-37 pins (`Has("_b1") && Has("_b2") && Has("_b3")`) pass. It is not only the callee-saved registers: `cmp/str r6,r1` (`in_r1_b1`, `in_r1_b2`, whose values are drawn) also `agrees` under the mutant, because a `cmp/str` is an OR of four byte compares and 32 inputs of which the edge values have equal bytes and the rest random 32-bit values (a byte pair equal with probability 1/256) almost never make exactly one of the swapped compares decide; `cmp/str r1,r2` swaps both sides alike. Only `r15` (`kStackTop`, bytes that differ) is caught. The names are the user's only way to tell which byte of a register a text means, and A2-25/A2-37 are about exactly that; no oracle pins their order. P3: the engine's numbering is right today (the unmutated engine's 165 piece-naming functions of the round 6 scan, every register r0-r14 and five instruction shapes, all `agrees`), the instrument is blind to a regression. | `Sh2GeneratedTests` printed-C: `CalleeSaved` with four different bytes, 44 inputs that share exactly one byte with a register a function may compare, r15 given the stack top the run gives it; the four hand words `286C 000B 0029;2E6C 000B 0029;216C 000B 0029;2F6C 000B 0029` agree unmutated and all four mismatch under the `registerPieceSuffix` mutant of the row | fixed 0fa9c49 (oracle only; the harness had also given the text r15 = CalleeSaved(15) where the run had the stack top, which agreed only while no input matched a byte of either) | area 2 round 6 | area 3 round 7 fixes |
| A2-45 | 2 | P3 | A value in r0 whose only other use is a store to a local that nothing reads is not returned, where the same value with a dead register use is. `mov r5,r0 ; add r0,r4 ; rts ; nop` (a dead use of r0) returns `param_2` and is lent `returns`; `add #-8,r15 ; mov r5,r0 ; mov.w r0,@(6,r15) ; add #8,r15 ; rts ; nop` (a dead store to the function's own frame, popped before the `rts`) prints `void func_0x06009000(void) { return; }` and is lent `known, 0 param(s), void`, dropping both the return and the parameter that was its value; a reload of the stored word into a dead register (`mov.l r0,@r15 ; mov.l @r15,r1`) is the same. The dead ALU use is removed before the engine decides the return, the dead stack store only after it (the STORE is still a use of r0 when `onlyOpUse` looks), so which of two equally dead uses counts is an accident of the order of the passes: A2-42's "stored" is a store the code needs, and this one it does not. Seen as the seed 2869 of the printed-C run's `void-return-lent` (`mov r5,r0 ; mov.w r0,@(6,r15)` with the frame popped). Unoptimised compiled code stores locals it never reads, hence P3. | none (the control `6053 340C 000B 0009` returns `param_2`; the two shapes of the row print `void` as before) | accepted: one rule with A3-41: a value in r0 that the function also uses for anything else is scratch, dead use or live. The dead ALU use is removed before the engine decides the return and the dead local store only after it (the store is still a use when `onlyOpUse` looks), so the two differ by the order of the passes; counting the dead ALU use would need dead-code removal ahead of return recovery, upstream's order, for a function that stores a value it never reads (unoptimised code) and whose caller, if it reads r0, is withheld ("uses r0 after calling") | area 2 round 6 | area 3 round 7 fixes |

| A5-70 | 5 | P3 | A5-60's closure of a pointer's own bitwise values is lost when the operation is conditional, or one of two in a turn of the loop, and both then print a wrong or missing note. Cause (`PointerResolver`, `Sh2Decompiler.cpp`): `Walk` closes `selfOps` at a MULTIEQUAL (`CloseSelfOps`) over the constants that merge itself holds. (1) An inner join of the loop body holds none (the first value sits at the loop head's other input), so `if (c) p = p and mask;` leaves `selfOps` in a merge with an empty set, which is cleared with nothing closed, and the head's merge sees only the first value; the usual order `read; update; loop` has the read use the head's phi, which is where it goes wrong (the same update before the read is closed: control below). (2) `Bitwise` takes an operand that is a node with `selfOps` and a cycle but no constant for `onlyCycle` and builds its result from the new operation alone, so the earlier operation of the same turn is dropped. (3) A step on the other path (`Shift` of a cycle value) makes the merge `others`, and `CloseSelfOps` then closes nothing. Fixtures, each replayable with `SE_GENERATED_WORDS="<words>"` (the literal pool is among the words; base 0x06009000). (a) `mov.l lit,r1 (0x25F80004); mov.l lit,r3 (0x1FFFFFFF); mov #3,r4; L: mov.w @r1,r0; tst r0,r0; bt skip; and r3,r1; skip: dt r4; bf L`: the emulator reads 0x05F80004 (the cached partition: a cache read) and 0x25F80004; the output is `puVar1 = &VDP2_TVSTAT; do { if (*puVar1 != 0) { puVar1 = (uint2 *)((uint4)puVar1 & 0x1fffffff); } ... }` with the one note `VDP2_TVSTAT: a read clears EXLTFG`, certain, no `if the pointer holds`, no cache caution; the generator replay reports `[n:cache-read-pointer-unsaid]`. Control (a'): the same with the conditional mask before the read (`tst r4,r4; bt skip; and r3,r1; skip: mov.w @r1,r0`) says `if the pointer holds 0x05F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT): a read of VDP2_TVSTAT through the cached partition is a cache read ...`. (b) two operations in one turn, `p = (p and 0xFFFFFFF0) xor 4` from 0x25F80008 (VDP2_HCNT): the reads are 0x25F80008, then 0x25F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT) for ever; the output prints `puVar2 = &VDP2_HCNT; ... puVar2 & 0xfffffff0 ^ 4` and no note at all (the TVSTAT read is unsaid, and the pointer is taken for certain). (c) the same with `and 0xFFFFFFF9`, `xor 4` from 0x25F80006 (VDP2_VRSIZE): the reads are 0x25F80006, then 0x25F80004; the output says `if the pointer holds 0x25F80002 (VDP2_EXTEN): VDP2_EXTEN: a read latches HCNT and VCNT while EXLTEN is 0`, a register the code never reads (the xor alone closes 0x25F80006 to 0x25F80002), and nothing of TVSTAT: a false note. (d) a mask on one path and a step on the other, `tst r0,r0; bt step; and r3,r1; bra join; nop; step: add #4,r1; join: dt r4; bf L`: the emulator reads 0x05F80004 and 0x25F80004, 0x25F80008, 0x25F8000C; the notes are `if the pointer holds 0x25F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT) (it may hold other values too)` and the stepped note, with no cache caution (`[n:cache-read-pointer-unsaid]`). (e) a step and a mask in one turn, the commonest form, `L: mov.w @r1,r0; add #4,r1; and r3,r1; dt r4; bf L` from 0x25F80004 with r3 = 0x1FFFFFFF: the emulator reads 0x25F80004, 0x05F80008 and 0x05F8000C; the output is `puVar2 = (uint2 *)((uint4)(puVar2 + 2) & 0x1fffffff)` with the two notes of the stepped pointer (`if the pointer holds 0x25F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT) (it may hold other values too)`, `the pointer is stepped in a loop from 0x25F80004: ... not said`) and no word on the cached partition (`[n:cache-read-pointer-unsaid]` twice). (it may hold other values too)`; (c) names 0x25F80006 and 0x25F80004 and not 0x25F80002. The fix is in the closure: keep every operation of the turn in `selfOps` (an earlier one is not dropped), carry `selfOps` through a merge that cannot close them (or close them against the values the loop head holds), and close over the constants even when the merge is `others`. The idiom is the cartridge probe, a games' RAM-cartridge check: store a pattern, read it back, compare. `mov.l lit,r1 (0x02400000); mov.w lit,r2 (0x1234); mov.w r2,@r1; mov.w @r1,r3; cmp/eq r2,r3; bt present; mov #1,r0; rts; nop; present: mov #0,r0; rts; nop` is printed `xRam02400000 = 0x1234; return 0;` with `Removing unreachable block (ram,0x0600900c)`: the absent-cartridge branch is gone and the function claims the cartridge is present; the same on a bare machine returns 1 (the read is 0xFFFF). No caution on the store (the BIOS ROM store has one). Not in either corpus image (no access to 0x02000000-0x04FFFFFF). | A5-79 | 5 | P3 | (Extends A5-68's argument; the reviewer asks the coordinator to decide.) The register list of A5-68 is the registers that re-address a memory the CPU reaches by address; `VDP1_FBCR` is one more. `vdp1.c` `Write8_DB` / `Write16_DB` / `Read16_DB` reach `VDP1_FB[FBDrawWhich]`, and `FBDrawWhich` is toggled at the frame end when `!(FBCR and FCM) or (FBManualPending and (FBCR and FCT))` (line 954): after a frame change the same address is the other buffer. A function that stores to the frame buffer, requests the change (`VDP1_FBCR = 3`, which `WriteEffects` says), waits for the frame end (a poll of VDP2_TVSTAT) and reads the address back has the read folded: `mov.l lit,r8 (0x25C80200); mov.w lit,r0 (0x1234); mov.w r0,@r8; mov.l lit,r1 (0x25D00002); mov #3,r2; mov.w r2,@r1; mov.l lit,r3 (0x25F80004); W: mov.w @r3,r0; tst #8,r0; bt W; mov.w @r8,r0; rts` is printed `xRam25c80200 = 0x1234; VDP1_FBCR = 3; do { uVar1 = VDP2_TVSTAT; } while ((uVar1 & 8) == 0); return 0x1234;`, and the read after the swap is of the other buffer. The same holds for the automatic swap (FCM = 0) with no FBCR write, and the drawing and erase the VDP1 does write the buffer, which is memory another agent writes and the user's to mark. The write to FBCR is evidence in the function, as TVMR is (`ConstAccess::remaps` bit 0). P3, and arguably a question: a function that reads the frame buffer after a swap is rare, and the asynchronous swap makes the barrier by position the honest one, which the data flow lacks (A5-68's own reason). Expected: the FB read after the `VDP1_FBCR` write is kept (frame buffer volatile in a function that writes FBCR with FCM set), or a note on the write that says addresses of the frame buffer mean the other buffer after the frame end. | A6-43 | 6 | P3 | A write that fails for want of room, met by an allocation failure while its error text is made, leaks the open file and leaves the staging directory. `WriteAndClose` (`FileWrite.cpp`) builds its message first (`error = Reason("could not write", path)`: a handful of strings) and calls `std::fclose(f)` after it: if one of those allocations throws, the `FILE*` and its descriptor are never closed, `WriteFileAtomically`'s `RemoveFile(temp)` never runs, and the `.separt-<pid>-<n>` temporary stays in the staging directory. In the `fclose` branch (`could not finish writing`) the descriptor is closed but the temporary stays the same way. `Extract`'s guard (A6-37) then unlinks its known paths, `rmdir` fails on the temporary, and it falls back to the allocating `RemoveEntry`: with one failed allocation that works (the directory goes; the FILE and descriptor stay), with an allocation failure that stays (sustained exhaustion) it cannot, and `<config>/decompiler/<hash>.tmp-<pid>` stays on disk (the three small files, the temporary, and the partial `sh-2.sla`); no later process removes another pid's. So A6-33 / A6-37's "removed on every exit but the move" holds for allocation failures only, which is all the matrix models: no family ever fails a write, `cold` never meets ENOSPC. A disk that is full AND an allocation that fails in a short string is rare, and the next request retries (A6-40) and succeeds, so P3: one `FILE` and a descriptor, or up to about 19 KB on disk, per event. Real ENOSPC alone is handled (a 24 KB tmpfs: Unavailable with the path, nothing left, Ok after `mount -o remount,size=1m`, the same process). | `<scratch> --diskfull [sticky]` (a hand mode of `SaturnExplorerDecompilerFaultTests`, `DiskFullProbe`): the first request of a process with `RLIMIT_FSIZE` at 5,000 bytes (the three small spec files fit, the 13 KB `sh-2.sla` does not: "directory made, three files written, the disk full on the fourth"; `SE_DF_LIMIT=<bytes>` moves it) and allocation k failing, for every k of that request (93 cells); then the limit is lifted and the next request must be Ok with no `.tmp-` entry in `<cfg>/decompiler` and no more descriptors than before. | A2-47 | 2 | P1 | `bt/s` (and, at the other alignment of the code, `bf/s`) with `mova` or `mov.l @(d,pc)` in its slot prints the address without its `& ~3` when the displacement is odd, so the taken path loads from (or returns) an address two bytes off: `4415 8D03 C704 000B 0009 0009 000B 0009` (`cmp/pl r4 ; bt/s +3 ; mova @(4,pc),r0 ; rts ; nop ; nop ; rts ; nop` at 0x06009000) returns 0x0600901C for r4 = 1 and the text is `iVar1 = (uint4)(0 < param_1) * 6 + 0x6009018 ; ... return iVar1;`, which evaluates to 0x0600901E; the `mov.l` form reads `*(xunknown4 *)((uint4)(0 < param_1) * 6 + 0x6009018)`, an unaligned longword. No warning. Patch 0050's p-code is right (the decode oracle: 0 differ) and the text is the engine's: the spec makes `t * (target + 2) + (1 - t) * (slot + 4)`, `& 0xfffffffc`, `+ 4 * disp`, `RuleCollectTerms` / `Funcdata::distributeIntMultAdd` rewrite the sum into `t * 6 + 0x6009008` by changing the inputs of an existing INT_ADD in place, whose output keeps the non-zero mask of the value it used to be (`(1 - t) * 0x6009008`: 0xfffffffc, found by printing the tree at `RuleAndMask::applyOp`: `+ nz=fffffffc local=ffffffff`), and `RuleAndMask` takes the AND with 0xfffffffc for redundant. Not particular to the slot: the same arithmetic written as code (`4415 0129 D206 D307 0217 061A E401 3418 0347 051A 365C E7FC 2679 6063 000B 0009 0600 900E 0600 9008`, r6 = T * 0x0600900E + (1 - T) * 0x06009008, r6 &= -4) prints `(uint4)(0 < param_1) * 6 + 0x6009008` and returns 0x0600900E for T = 1 where the code returns 0x0600900C. In a sweep of bt/s and bf/s with a mova slot (displacement 1 to 6, the code at 0x06009002 and 0x06009004) every odd displacement of one of the two is wrong, the even ones agree; `mov.w` has no alignment and agrees. The generator cannot draw it (no PC-relative slot load), the access ledger is by address only, and the hand fixtures held the text to a substring. Experiment, not a fix: setting `op->getOut()->nzm = ~0` in `Funcdata::opSetInput` when an input is replaced prints the mask back (`((uint4)(0 < param_1) * 6 + 0x6009008 & 0xfffffffc) + 0x10`), keeps the rest of `Sh2DecompilerTests` green and turns every pin below round. | `TestArea2Round7SlotValues` (`Sh2DecompilerTests.cpp`): evaluates the printed address of the slot load against the run for bt/s and bf/s x mova, mov.w, mov.l x even and odd displacement, rts + mova, the control `6 * T` as code (the mask stays) and the A2-47 control; each case is now held to the run (bt/s and bf/s x mova, mov.w, mov.l x even and odd, with the branch at both alignments of the code, 24 cases) and the odd mova and mov.l ones to the `& 0xfffffffc` in the text; the control is held to the run too (reverting patch 0046 fails 8 CHECKs) | fixed ad80a00 (patch 0046: `Funcdata::opSetInput` sets the non-zero mask of the output of the op whose input it replaces back to every-bit-possible, so `ActionNonzeroMask` recomputes it before a rule reads it; the narrowest place that covers every rule that rewrites an op in place, and the cause is the mask not the rule: `RuleCollectTerms` puts the sum into an inner ADD that was a multiple of 4. Corpus: 10 of 839 and 24 of 1,554 default-request texts change, all value-preserving: section "Area 2, round 7 fix") | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 fix |
| A2-48 | 2 | P2 | A delayed branch translated twice in a row paints its slot's context on the instruction after the slot. `Sleigh::oneInstruction` applies the commits of the branch's parse tree (`pos->applyCommits()`, which evaluates `globalset(inst_next, ...)` from the tree's next address) and then moves that next address to the end of the delay slot (`pos->setNaddr(...+ fallOffset)`); when the second translation finds the tree in the cache (the ring of two of A2-5, LRU since patch 0020) the commits are applied again with `inst_next` now the address after the slot, so the slot's `slot_pc_valid` / `slot_tgt` (bra, bsr: patch 0005) or `slot_kind` / `slot_rm` (every other kind: patch 0050) are set on the instruction that follows it instead. A PC-relative load or mova there reads the branch's PC: `bsr ; nop ; mova @(3,pc),r0` at 0x06026408 makes `r0 = 0x06026428` after `T(bsr) ; T(bsr)` where the hardware gives 0x06026424. 215 of 240 cases (60 pairs: ten delayed branches x mov.l, mov.w, mova x two alignments, four runs each) differ after two translations, 0 of 240 after one; bra and bsr too, so the defect is older than patch 0050. The decode oracle cannot see it (its cells hold a nop after the slot and translate each branch once: `translation history` 0 of 5,632 differ). Product reach: none found. The emulator translates every instruction it executes (`EmulatePcodeCache::createInstruction` keeps no cache), so a tight loop of one delayed branch and its slot (`L: bf/s L ; dt r2`) poisons the instruction after the loop, but the exit of a bt/s or bf/s loop is the not-taken path, where the polluted p-code computes the ordinary PC from T; `ReturnCheck::Step` translates every instruction again and a branch twice in a row is rare. Experiment, not a fix: `pos->setNaddr(pos->getAddr() + pos->getLength())` before `applyCommits()` in `oneInstruction` makes the count 0, with the rest of the oracle unchanged. | `Sh2DecodeTests` `RunFollowers`: 60 pairs after one translation of the branch (must be 0, is 0), after two in a row (now `== 0`; reverting patch 0047 makes it 215 of 240); `SE_DECODE_FORM=<name>` selects the pairs | fixed ad80a00 (patch 0047: `Sleigh::oneInstruction` sets the tree's next address to the end of the branch itself before applying the commits, then to the end of the slot as before) | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 fix |
| A2-49 | 2 | P3 | A function that returns the value a lent callee returned is printed `void` and lent `void` when that value is also compared by a branch whose two arms do nothing: `2F86 2F96 2FC6 0C2A 7FF8 0009 0009 2F36 0009 63F6 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 D309 430B 0009 0009 0009 2008 8903 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 7F08 4C2A 6CF6 69F6 000B 68F6 0600 9054 0009 0009 7FC0 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 0009 624C 0009 0009 7F40 000B 6023` (`SE_GENERATED_FUNCS="0/0/4c,54/54/78" SE_GENERATED_RETURNS="1,1"`: the first function pushes r8, r9, r12, calls the second through `jsr @r3`, then `tst r0,r0 ; bt` over nothing, pops and returns; the second returns `extu.b r4`) prints `void func_0x06009000(xunknown1 param_1) { func_0x06009054(param_1); return; }`, lent `known, 1 param(s), void`, where r0 holds the callee's result. The same function without the `tst r0,r0 ; bt` pair returns it (and the one-call minimal form `4F22 D305 430B 0009 2008 8900 0009 4F26 000B 0009 0009 0009 0600 901C 6043 000B 0009` does too): the comparison's branch has nothing on either side and is removed, but while the engine decides the return it is a use of r0 (A2-45's order of the passes, with a flag as the use). A caller that reads r0 afterwards is withheld ("uses r0 after calling"), so nothing is lost silently; the generator's `return-dropped` class (A2-34) counts it as a failure, seed 62956 of grammar 2 (the first seed beyond the nightly's 20,000 where it shows: 1 in 6,000). Accept like A2-45, or fix with it. | `SE_GENERATED_WORDS`/`..._FUNCS`/`..._RETURNS` as written (the generator's own shrink of the seed, `... 7F08 4C2A 6CF6 69F6 68F6 000B 0009`, is not a counterexample: A2-50) | accepted: one rule with A2-45 and A3-41, and not a fix to make cheaply: the lent callee's 1-byte result reaches the RETURN as a zero-extension of a piece of a concatenation of r0's other bytes, not as the CALL's own output (which patch 0044 takes as a return whatever else it is used for), so the compare of r0 is still a use of it when the return is decided; the branch whose arms do nothing (their dead writes go first, the empty branch later) is removed after that decision, and counting it would need dead-code and branch removal ahead of return recovery, or the extension and the concatenation looked through, for a function that compares a value and does nothing with the answer (unoptimised code). A caller that reads r0 afterwards is withheld ("uses r0 after calling"), so nothing is lost silently. The generator counts the shape (`excluded.call-result-compared.return-dropped`) instead of failing on it, pinned by `TestRound7Pins` (seed 62956, the reduction in this row, and a control) | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 fix |
| A2-50 | 2 | P3 | (Oracle.) The generator's shrinker keeps the function's declared intent after it has removed the instruction that made it true, so a shrunk replay can fail with no counterexample in it: seed 62956's `[n:return-dropped]` shrinks to `2F86 2F96 2FC6 0C2A 7FF8 6843 6953 7F08 4C2A 6CF6 69F6 68F6 000B 0009` with `SE_GENERATED_RETURNS="1"`, a function that never writes r0 and is rightly lent `void`; the report prints it as "shrunk from the first finding". A reader replaying the shrunk line is told of a defect that is not there (the original line, the finding's `replay:` of the unshrunk function, is the counterexample; the 60-word reduction in A2-49 was made by replacing words with nops while the callee still returned and the call stayed). The `returns` / `intent` flags need re-validating against a run (r0 written on every path by an instruction still in the function) after each shrink step, or the finding classes that read them (`return-dropped`, `return-dropped:partial`) should shrink only items that cannot change the fact. | seed 62956 grammar 2 | fixed ad80a00 (the shrinker refuses a step that leaves `return g(p);` without its call, `Shrinker::IntentHolds`, and `return-dropped` needs r0 written on every finished run; seed 62956 shrunk with the exclusion off keeps its call and compare and is a counterexample; pins `TestRound7Pins`) | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 fix |
| A2-51 | 2 | P3 | (Oracle.) The decode oracle fails at other seeds than its default with cases it "could not run": at `SE_DECODE_SEED=555`, 2 million cases of each, jsr 8, jmp 4, bsrf 6 (and one jsr at seed 31337, 100,000 per form), "Could not obtain cached delay slot instruction" while translating an odd address. `PrepMem` takes the `@Rn` of jmp and jsr for a memory operand of width 1 (`OpDef::mem` is 0 for them) and overwrote the jump register `PickTarget` had just set with any address, odd ones included, and the slot's own `PrepMem` (or, since A2-46, its `RelateOperands`) can do the same to a branch register of any kind. An odd target is an address error on the hardware (A2-8), and the emulator translates code at an odd address, the middle of two instructions of the image: a slot-illegal word there fails the translation. The default seed and the CI runs never meet it; a reviewer's other seed does. The register's value (even, near the code or at the ends of the space) is what the case was meant to test, and the old override replaced it for jmp and jsr in most cases. | `SE_DECODE_FORM="jsr @Rn" SE_DECODE_CASES=2000000 SE_DECODE_SEED=555` on the base: 8 could not run | fixed 0cc677a (oracle only: the branch register is restored after both `PrepMem` calls and the widening; the failing example lines now carry the slot, the address being translated and the registers) | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 |
